- [x] 23. Secrets are zeroized everywhere they are copied
  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings: decrypted and downloaded secrets, RCI secret byte buffers, the copied preset, raw-form secrets (no derived Debug, `SecretString`), W5-13: fix the gap in kcc-migrate's `Preset`/`PresetPayload` model (serialize from borrowed `&str` into one `SecretString`, deserialize into `Zeroizing<Vec<String>>`), not as a deviation; and preset sources (F-14: keep the startup configuration in `SecretBytes`, and unify the two `--from` parsers into one). Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `SEC2-16` (low; critic confirmed) Gaps in zeroizing decrypted and downloaded secrets. Evidence: `crates/kcc-app/src/secrets.rs:277-285`, `kcc-config/src/ns3.rs:43-49,67-69`, `kcc-rci/src/engine.rs:698-711`, `engine.rs:561`. Fix: Wrap the buffers in `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>`. Pre-size `read_capped` from `Content-Length` or zeroize on growth.
  - `A-10` (low; critic adjusted) Secret bytes are copied into buffers that are never zeroized. Evidence: `crates/kcc-rci/src/engine.rs:698-710`, `crates/kcc-rci/src/session.rs:622`, `crates/kcc-config/src/ns3.rs:43-69`, `crates/kcc-catalog/src/runtime.rs:219`. Fix: Read into `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>` with a capacity reserved up front. Pass `Bytes` built from the secret without an extra copy. Zeroize ns3 buffers on every path. Move the value into `capture` instead of cloning it. Critic correction (binding): Limit the finding to `read_capped` (and `SecretSlice::from(Vec)`, which can reallocate), the multipart `to_vec`, and the ns3 error path.
  - `W5-13` (low; critic adjusted) The copied preset is held in a plain `Vec<String>` that is never zeroized. Evidence: `presets_page/saved.rs:41-50,58-62,85-87`. Fix: Serialize from borrowed `&str` straight into one `SecretString`, and deserialize into `Zeroizing<Vec<String>>`. Critic correction (binding): The rule (d) gap is in kcc-migrate's `Preset`/`PresetPayload` model; `saved.rs` only inherits it. Fix it there, or call it a model-wide deviation.
  - `W4-09` (low; critic confirmed) Raw-form secrets are held in plain `String`s with derived `Debug`. Evidence: `rci_check.rs:24-34`, `cli.rs:50-54`, `request.rs:161-162`, `vpn_import.rs:258-261`. Fix: Implement a manual `Debug` that omits `body`/`line`. Zeroize the `pairs`/`Bytes` copies, or move the text straight into `SecretString`/`SecretValue`, as `secret_pages::dialog::take` does.
  - `F-14` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) Two parsers for `--from`, and preset sources copy the startup configuration into a plain `Vec<u8>`. Evidence: `preset/source.rs:120-130`, `migrate_plan.rs:127-163`, `source.rs:97-100`, `source.rs:94`. Fix: One `--from` parser and reader returning `SecretBytes`, shared by migrate and preset.
  Must NOT: change unrelated behaviour, add a crate that is not already in `[workspace.dependencies]` or `Cargo.lock`, weaken a security control, change the audit entry format or hash rule, or touch files owned by another open todo of the same wave beyond what this todo needs; keep routes, MCP tool names/schemas and CLI flags except where a finding above changes them on purpose (name each such change in the commit body).

  What to do / Must NOT do: Second critic's correction applies: in `PresetStore::capture`/`save` read the existing record and require `authorize_source` on ITS `source_router` (the check `delete` makes) before replacing it (do NOT use `create_new`: it would break recapture by authorized users); `get`/`delete` authorize before revealing existence (same error for missing and forbidden); the MCP and CLI capture paths use the same rule. Tests: a lab-scoped Migrate key cannot replace an office preset; missing and forbidden ids answer identically. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `E-02` (high; critic confirmed) `kcc_preset_capture` overwrites any stored preset by name slug, skipping the source-scope check. Evidence: `crates/kcc-mcp/src/preset_tools/capture.rs:152-156,190-193`, `crates/kcc-migrate/src/presets/store.rs:120-129`, `kcc-web/src/presets_page/compose.rs:127`. Fix: Have `PresetStore::capture` refuse an existing id (or require the old record's source scope plus an explicit replace flag). In MCP, either give the slug a random suffix or return `conflict` when the id exists. Critic correction (binding): n/a. The root cause is in `PresetStore::capture`, so the store-level fix covers the CLI too.
  - `G-02` (medium; critic adjusted, high->medium) Preset capture silently overwrites any existing preset, including one outside the caller's router scope. Evidence: `crates/kcc-migrate/src/presets/store.rs:120-129`, `crates/kcc-mcp/src/preset_tools/capture.rs:155`. Fix: in `capture`/`save`, refuse when `self.path(id)` exists (`create_new`), or read the existing record and require `authorize_source` on its `source_router`, the same check `delete` does. Critic correction (binding): Vector: an MCP identity with `Migrate` on any router can overwrite and destroy a preset of another scope by naming its capture so the slug matches. Fix as proposed: `create_new`, or `authorize_source` on the existing record.

- [x] 26. Authorization gaps: CLI dominance, job ownership, role deletion locks, RevealSecrets gate
  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings: CLI `apikey create`/`user add` enforce role dominance like the Web; only the job's owner or an admin can stop/follow a job, and job ids are unpredictable; role deletion counts holders under the users/API-key locks; delete the unused vpn-server `RevealSecrets` gate (D-14) and its test; route-table scope checks and handlers use the same decoded router id. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `F-06` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) CLI `apikey create` and `user add` skip the role-dominance check the Web enforces. Evidence: `crates/kcc/src/cli/api_keys.rs:31-33`, `accounts.rs:42-48`, `kcc-web/src/admin_keys.rs:212`. Fix: Move `dominates` into `kcc_app::api_keys::create_api_key` and `AccountStore::create` (taking the actor's role), or call it from the CLI before both. Critic correction (binding): This is a consistency issue, not a security escalation.
  - `W4-02` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) Any reader of a router can stop or follow other users' jobs, and job ids are predictable. Evidence: `advanced/jobs.rs:214-219`, `advanced/jobs.rs:187-196`, `system_pages/jobs.rs:244-251`, `job_stream.rs:72-81`. Fix: Store the starting username (or session key) next to the job id: in `keep_output` and in `JobFeeds::follow`. Require it to match in `explorer_job`, `followed`, both `cancel` handlers and both streams, or require an admin permission to act on other users' jobs. Critic correction (binding): This is a missing ownership check on read-only background jobs. A router-scoped viewer can cancel or watch other users' diagnostics and explorer read jobs, and the ids are easy to enumerate. Write jobs, raw jobs and the commit job are out of reach. The proposed fix (store the starting user, require a match or an admin permission) is sound.
  - `W1-17` (low; critic confirmed) Deleting a role counts holders without the users/API-key locks. Evidence: `crates/kcc-web/src/admin_roles.rs:631-637,648-650`. Fix: Validate the role name inside the users and API-key update closures as well, or take the 3 locks in a fixed order.
  - `D-14` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) `RevealSecrets` gate is shipped with no production caller, and its scope check is not tied to the router. Evidence: `crates/kcc-domain/src/vpn_servers/secrets.rs:38-41`, `lib.rs:61-66`, `tests/vpn_server_secrets.rs:47,89`. Fix: Delete it until a surface needs it, or bind the router id inside `RouterCtx` and record an audit entry inside the gate. Critic correction (binding): This is dead, latent API with a scope check that is not bound to the router. Delete it or bind the router id. Its present cost is low.
  - `SEC1-14` (info; critic confirmed) Route-table scope checks use the raw path, handlers use the decoded one. Evidence: `crates/kcc-web/src/authz.rs:268-281`, `page_kit/context.rs:181-195`, `action.rs:91-106`, `workflow.rs:602`. Fix: Percent-decode the segment before `registry::authorize`, or rely only on the handler check.

- [x] 27. Audit appends are O(1) and survive a torn last line (M19, M20)
  What to do / Must NOT do: Under the cross-process lock read only the tail of the active file (the last complete line) to get `seq` and the previous hash, as the critic says - no sidecar file; full verification stays in `kcc audit verify` and at startup. A torn last line (crash or full disk) is detected at writer spawn AND before each append, moved to `audit.jsonl.torn-<unix-ts>`, logged and audited as a repair event; the chain continues from the last complete entry. Remove the duplicated helpers and dead state (B-14). Must not change the entry format or hash rule. Tests (no test-only hooks in production code): make an old rotated audit file unreadable and show that appends still succeed and stay chained; a truncated last line is repaired and `kcc audit verify` passes afterwards; multiprocess ordering still holds. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `B-01` (medium; critic adjusted, high->medium) Every audit append re-reads, re-parses and re-hashes the whole retained log, under the cross-process lock. Evidence: `crates/kcc-app/src/audit.rs:582-597`, `audit.rs:750-783`, `writer.rs:46-57`, `audit.rs:444-449`. Fix: Under the lock, read only the tail of the newest file (seek back to the last newline) to get `seq` and `hash`. Keep full verification for `verify_chain` and startup. Detect rotation by another process with file identity and size checks. Critic correction (binding): The O(retained log) append under the cross-process lock is real and contradicts `SECURITY.md:315`. The latency and memory figures are unproven. The fix stays as proposed: read the tail of the newest file under the lock and keep full verification for `verify_chain` and startup.
  - `SEC2-05` (medium; critic confirmed) Every audit append re-reads and re-hashes the entire retained chain (O(N) per entry). Evidence: `crates/kcc-app/src/audit.rs:587-588`. Fix: Verify the full chain only at startup or `kcc audit verify`. Before each append, read only the tail of the active file (or the last rotated file when the active one is empty) to get `seq` and `prev_hash`.
  - `B-03` (medium; critic confirmed) A torn last audit line (crash or full disk mid-append) stops `kcc serve` from starting and kills the writer. Evidence: `crates/kcc-app/src/audit.rs:621-624`, `audit.rs:761-762`, `audit.rs:444-445`, `crates/kcc/src/server.rs:365-369`. Fix: On open, if the last line has no trailing `\n`, truncate the file back to the last newline and log it (an incomplete line has no hash to protect). Treat only complete lines that fail verification as tampering.
  - `SEC2-04` (medium; critic confirmed) One torn or partial audit line permanently stops the audit writer and stops `kcc serve` from starting. Evidence: `crates/kcc-app/src/audit.rs:587-588`, `audit/writer.rs:71-74`. Fix: Treat an unterminated last line of the active file as torn. Truncate it, or move it to a quarantine file and log that, before verifying. Do this both in `spawn_with_policy` and before each append.
  - `B-14` (low; critic confirmed) Audit module: duplicated helpers, dead state and unused public API. Evidence: `audit.rs:692-704`, `audit.rs:890-904`, `persist.rs:676-691`, `audit.rs:446-462`. Fix: Delete the dead builder and fields, reuse one reader and one JSON-error mapper, and gate test-only constructors behind a test feature.

- [x] 28. Audit waits are truly bounded; request ids unique; retention as documented (M21)
  What to do / Must NOT do: B-04: keep ONE unbounded channel to the writer, so the single-queue write order of `crates/kcc-app/src/workflow_receipts.rs:10-12` holds, plus a 128-permit semaphore that only bounded callers take, under their own limit (`timeout(limit, permits.acquire())`), released when the writer has persisted the entry. Add a separate bounded entry point next to `submit` (for example `submit_within(event, limit)`) that takes the permit and then submits; `AuditLog::append` as a whole does not become bounded. Exactly the two audit helpers in `crates/kcc-web/src/auth.rs` use the bounded entry point: the shared `audit` helper (`:463-474`, today `append`; it serves failed login, logout and password change, which all act on the result) and `audit_login` (`:486-490`, which calls `submit` directly today; it moves to the bounded entry point under `LOGIN_AUDIT_WAIT`). Every other caller (for example `crates/kcc/src/server_migrations.rs:35` and `crates/kcc-migrate/src/apply/agreements.rs:77`) stays on `submit`/`append`, whose wait is already capped by `AUDIT_WAIT` once `submit` no longer blocks. After their limit they get a NEW, distinct timeout error (not `AuditError::Pending`, which `audit_login` maps to `Ok` at `auth.rs:490-492`) and handle it exactly as they handle an audit write error today, so a login is refused, never signed in without an entry. Take no permit, never block and never drop (owner rule: audit entries wait for the lock and are written later): every `Receipts::record` submission, deferred or not (`crates/kcc-app/src/workflow_receipts.rs:43-58`; the router change has already happened, `workflow.rs:1023,1098,1331,1398`), and the callers that only log an audit error, the job-recovery audit (`crates/kcc-app/src/jobs.rs:593-595`) and the MCP failure audit (`crates/kcc-mcp/src/support/audit.rs:105-107`), so none of them can lose an entry on a saturated queue. The same no-permit rule covers the callers of `submit` that only log or drop the result: the Web action audit (`crates/kcc-web/src/page_kit/audit.rs:141-161`; the production shutdown path only calls `audit.flush`, and `crates/kcc/src/server_shutdown.rs:186` is a test that keeps working). So the permit is taken only in the bounded entry point, NEVER inside `submit` itself, which would drop Web audit entries on a saturated queue. Accepted cost (owner never-drop rule): while another process holds the append lock, a flood of no-permit entries (for example failing MCP calls) grows the queue in memory; state this in the rustdoc of the queue. Nothing in a safety bracket's recovery path (`SafetyNet::disarm`, the rollback) waits on the audit queue. Tests: a bounded append on a saturated queue returns its timeout within the limit; a receipt submitted while saturated is written once the writer drains, in order; a bracket disarms with a saturated queue; workflow audit entries get unique request ids (one shared id generator replacing the five Unix-time helpers, F-25), and terminal changes keep their receipt (B-20); correct the rustdoc of the retention setting to describe the real behaviour (B-15: the critic rated it a wording issue). Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `B-04` (medium; critic confirmed) "Bounded" audit waits block without limit once the 128-slot queue is full, including inside a safety bracket. Evidence: `crates/kcc-app/src/audit.rs:452`, `workflow_receipts.rs:48`, `crates/kcc-domain/src/configuration/bracket.rs:113-127`, `crates/kcc-web/src/auth.rs:486-490`. Fix: Put the reserve under the caller's limit, e.g. `timeout(limit, sender.reserve())`. For deferred receipts, use an unbounded queue or `try_reserve` and return a `Pending` that reports "not queued".
  - `B-20` (low; critic adjusted) Workflow audit entries use timestamps as request ids, and terminal changes drop their audit receipt. Evidence: `workflow.rs:1022`, `workflow.rs:438-444`. Fix: Pass the caller's request id into the `ChangeSet`, and keep `audit` on `TerminalCliChangeResult`. Critic correction (binding): Keep the request-id issue. The dropped field does not stop callers from reporting a pending entry.
  - `F-25` (low; critic confirmed) Unix-time helper copied five times; audit request ids are not unique. Evidence: `context.rs:426,447`, `server_migrations.rs:25-27`, `api_keys.rs:134-138`, `preset/source.rs:61-63`. Fix: Use `SystemClock::unix_seconds()` and a random or sequenced request id.
  - `B-15` (info; critic adjusted, low->info) Audit retention runs only on rotation and only on whole files, unlike its documentation. Evidence: `settings.rs:272`, `audit.rs:615-620`. Fix: Correct the doc, or also prune at spawn time and on a timer. Critic correction (binding): This is a rustdoc wording fix only. The user docs match the code.

- [x] 29. Audit page respects router scope and streams instead of loading the whole log
  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings: filter entries by the viewer's router scope (entries without a router stay visible only to unscoped roles) and page through the log without verifying and loading it whole on every view: verify once and cache the result keyed by the file's length and mtime (W1-08 critic), paginate the entries before formatting rows, and stream the export body. Fix: Drop entries whose `router` is set and outside `user.role().router_scope` (`scope_matches`), in both `page` and `export`. Either filter entries by `scope_matches(role.router_scope, entry.router)` in `page` and `export`, or document ViewAudit as fleet-wide.

Fix: Verify once and cache the result by file length and mtime. Format rows only for the page slice (paginate the `AuditEntry` list first). Stream the export body. Paginating before `row`, and caching the verification result by file length and mtime, are the cheap fixes.

- [x] 30. Every successful or partial action is audited with a traceable request id
  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings: MCP actions that Web and CLI audit are audited; dropped receipts are reported (`audit_pending`); a CLI batch whose later line fails writes a workflow entry with applied/failed counts like `apply_batch` (N2); a wizard commit/rollback the router accepted is audited even if the record save fails; wizard refusals and failures are audited like the kit; CLI local-state permission refusals are audited; logged failure causes carry the request id returned to the client. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `E-08` (medium; critic confirmed) Several successful MCP actions are not audited, although the Web and CLI audit the same actions. Evidence: `crates/kcc-mcp/src/config_tools/backups.rs:82-87`, `generic/fleet.rs:216-220`, `preset_tools/capture.rs:190-207`, `preset_tools/manage.rs:70-76`. Fix: Append a success `AuditEvent` on these paths, as the Web/CLI equivalents do.
  - `E-24` (low; critic adjusted) Some write paths drop the audit receipt, so `audit_pending` is never reported. Evidence: `crates/kcc-mcp/src/domains/write/vpn.rs:182-192`, `vpn_servers.rs:119-134`, `clients.rs:303-315,324-335`, `system.rs:87-97,103-122`. Fix: Return `common::applied(result.unsaved, &result.audit)` on every path. Critic correction (binding): Fix the system paths in MCP. The VPN-secret paths first need the domain functions to return their change result. ## Summary - CONFIRMED: 14 (E-02, E-04, E-05, E-07, E-08, E-12, E-13, E-14, E-15, E-16, E-17, E-18, E-20, E-22) - ADJUSTED: 9 (E-01, E-03, E-06, E-09, E-10, E-11, E-19, E-23, E-24) - REFUTED: 1 (E-21) - DUPLICATE: 0 **NEW-1 (high, to validate separately).** A Read user in the Web explorer can read `show running-config` with secrets intact, the same leak as E-01: - Evidence: `kcc-web/src/advanced/execute.rs:68-74,100` (a Read-risk command needs only `command_permissions(Risk::Read)`), then `deliver.rs:98-106` (`read_root`), then `viewer.rs:73-118`. There, `mask` replaces only ...
  - `N2` (low; second critic adjusted) A CLI batch whose later line fails writes no workflow entry recording the lines already applied. Binding description: A partial CLI batch is audited as a plain failure by the front end, with no record of which lines were applied. The fix is to record partial completion the way `apply_batch` does.
  - `W2-03` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) A wizard commit or rollback that succeeded on the router is not audited if the record save fails. Evidence: `wizard/finish.rs:243-251`, `finish.rs:275-280`. Fix: Append the audit entry before `end`, or append it on the error path of `end` too. Critic correction (binding): Only the wizard's finish summary entry is skipped when the record can't be saved; the router operations stay audited. Fix as proposed: append before `end` or on its error path.
  - `W2-10` (low; critic confirmed) Wizard refusals and failures are not audited the way the kit audits them. Evidence: `wizard/visit.rs:64-69`, `running.rs:511-513`, `finish.rs:66-68`, `settle.rs:469-471`. Fix: Append a `forbidden` record (the wizard's action, run id, target) in `Visit::open`'s `Forbidden` branch, and records for the 409 and 500 refusals in `finish`.
  - `F-13` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) Permission refusals on local-state commands are not audited. Evidence: `persist.rs:276-277`, `persist.rs:150,175,199,266`, `routers.rs:42-44,72`, `router_secrets.rs:35-37,105-107`. Fix: Route every refusal through one `Ctx::authorize_audited(services, perms, routers)` helper.
  - `E-09` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) Logged failure causes cannot be tied to the `request_id` returned to the client. Evidence: `crates/kcc-mcp/src/support/shape.rs:61-65`, `support/errors.rs:49,70`, `rmcp-3.9.0/src/service.rs:1606-1630`. Fix: Mint the id in `route()` / the `#[tool]` wrappers before the work and open a `tracing::info_span!("mcp_tool", request_id, tool)` around it. Critic correction (binding): n/a (severity only)

- [ ] 31. Conflict check by checksum; disarm skips the write preflight; dead last_read removed
  What to do / Must NOT do: `check_conflict` compares the checksum alone against the change set's expected state (critic-endorsed); remove the unused `last_read` parameter of `ChangeWorkflow::begin*` and its callers (N1); the bracket disarm uses a dedicated recovery path that skips the backup/conflict preflight so a local refusal can never block cleanup (C-02), and its failure is reported precisely. Update docs/ARCHITECTURE.md step 6 wording. Tests: a change by another RCI client (same agent, different checksum) is refused; a disarm after a local refusal is still sent. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `B-02` (medium; critic adjusted) The conflict check needs both checksum and agent to differ, so a change by any other RCI client goes unnoticed. Evidence: `crates/kcc-app/src/workflow.rs:692-700`, `crates/kcc-testkit/src/config_state.rs:42`, `workflow.rs:1429`, `tests/workflow.rs:91-108`. Fix: Compare `checksum` alone against kcc's own last observed state, which already tracks kcc's own writes. If kcc's own changes from other sets must be allowed, compare `(agent, user)` against kcc's login rather than agent alone. Critic correction (binding): This is a plan-mandated rule that does not deliver the README/ARCHITECTURE promise ("refuses if someone else changed the router"). In `check_conflict`, the agent clause protects nothing from kcc's own writes, so use checksum only, as `ensure_unchanged` already does. Separately, the "since the actor's last read" check is unused (see NEW-1).
  - `N1` (low; second critic adjusted) The `last_read` parameter of `ChangeWorkflow::begin*` is never used in production (every caller passes `None`). Binding description: The `last_read` parameter of `begin` is unused in production. Either remove it or thread a last-change marker from the page or plan read. The documented conflict check is unaffected.
  - `C-02` (medium; critic confirmed) The bracket's disarm runs the full write preflight, so a local refusal blocks cleanup and is reported as "may be armed". Evidence: `crates/kcc-domain/src/configuration.rs:271-273`, `kcc-app/src/workflow.rs`, `recovery.rs:113-116`. Fix: Send the disarm (and keep-alive) through a path that skips the conflict and backup preflight and keeps the authorization. Have `arm_failed` report `None` when the arm failed before anything was sent.
  Must NOT: change unrelated behaviour, add a crate that is not already in `[workspace.dependencies]` or `Cargo.lock`, weaken a security control, change the audit entry format or hash rule, or touch files owned by another open todo of the same wave beyond what this todo needs; keep routes, MCP tool names/schemas and CLI flags except where a finding above changes them on purpose (name each such change in the commit body).

- [ ] 32. Bracket and close outcomes are reported truthfully (M28-M31)
  What to do / Must NOT do: `guarded` refuses (does not replace) when a recovery timer is already armed by the operator (C-03), reporting it; refusals carry the right error and audit failures are not dropped (C-21); `close(Save)` in router mode does not send a second save and reports correctly (C-15); fix the `close` doc (C-16); finish failures report 'armed' when the timer is armed (G-05); the component commit job completes only after the router is back (C-11). Tests per finding. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `C-03` (medium; critic confirmed) `guarded` replaces and then cancels a recovery timer the operator armed earlier. Evidence: `crates/kcc-domain/src/configuration/bracket.rs:112-137`, `configuration.rs:103-121`. Fix: When the baseline shows `fail-safe.active == true`, either refuse the bracket or run the work without arming or disarming and leave the existing timer to its owner.
  - `C-21` (low; critic confirmed) The bracket reports refusals with the wrong error and drops an audit failure. Evidence: `crates/kcc-domain/src/configuration/bracket.rs:109-110`. Fix: Add a dedicated "unowned" variant, and fold the settle error into the arm failure.
  - `C-15` (low; critic confirmed) `close(Save)` in router mode sends a second save after the commit and can misreport the result. Evidence: `crates/kcc-domain/src/configuration/close.rs:82-83`, `workflow.rs:1180-1188`. Fix: Drop the second save in router mode, or verify `unsaved == false` instead.
  - `C-16` (info; critic adjusted, low->info) `close` doc gives the wrong fate for a failed keep-unsaved. Evidence: `close.rs:55-56`, `recovery.rs:128,155-158`. Fix: Correct the doc to `Applied`.
  - `G-05` (medium; critic adjusted) Finish failures report "timer not armed" while the timer is still armed. Evidence: `crates/kcc-migrate/src/apply/finish.rs:32-39`, `failure.rs:113-133`, `crates/kcc-web/src/wizard/finish.rs:232`. Fix: map every pre-close failure in `commit`, `keep_unsaved` and `rollback` (including `ensure_unchanged`) to an armed variant: `FinishFailed`, or a new `Drift`-with-armed fate. Critic correction (binding): `Drift` comes from `commit` and `keep_unsaved`, not from `rollback`. The harmful cases are commit and keep; rollback's misreport is cosmetic.
  - `C-11` (medium; critic confirmed) The component commit job reports "completed" before the router reboots. Evidence: `crates/kcc-domain/src/components/commit.rs:66-81`. Fix: Require one failed probe (down) before accepting a successful one, and compare the components against what was staged.

- [ ] 33. Router leases and wizard runs are released and bounded (M32, M33)
  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings: an abandoned or timed-out Web apply and an applied MCP migration/preset release the router lease through a lease timeout tied to the armed recovery timer plus eviction of closed records (no new cross-identity finish path); the global migrations mutex is not held across router commit/rollback; wizard session eviction cannot break per-run mutual exclusion and never drops a session in use; W5-06: at most 10 unfinished runs per owner and wizard (migration, presets; another start is refused with a localized message); an unfinished run whose last change is more than one day old counts as abandoned (the one-day window of `presets_page/saved.rs:27` `TRANSIENT_SECONDS`); finished and abandoned run directories are deleted 30 days after their last change: extend the sweep in `crates/kcc-web/src/presets_page/saved.rs:118-136` (today it only discards the sealed parts of copy runs) to every preset run, and add the same sweep for migration-wizard runs, which have none; landing pages read a per-owner index instead of every run. Document both numbers in docs/USER_GUIDE.md. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `W5-01` (medium; critic confirmed) An abandoned or timed-out Apply leaves the router claimed for the rest of the process's life. Evidence: `crates/kcc-web/src/migrate_wizard/apply.rs:75-88`, `brackets.rs:142-150`, `migrate_wizard/launch.rs:53`, `presets_page/launch.rs:60`. Fix: Wrap the claim in a guard whose `Drop` releases it unless it is disarmed after `start_run` has been saved. Do the reads before claiming, and call `brackets::hold()` right before `Applier::start`.
  - `E-03` (medium; critic adjusted) An applied migration or preset holds the router write lease indefinitely, and records are never evicted. Evidence: `crates/kcc-mcp/src/migration_tools/state.rs:38-39,69-74,177-190`, `kcc-migrate/src/apply.rs:70-71`, `kcc-app/src/persist.rs:277-282`, `preset_tools/state.rs:63-71`. Fix: Give records a TTL tied to the armed timer: after the router's timer window, close and drop the `Applier`. Evict unapplied plans by age and count. Allow a `Destructive` holder on the target to finish another identity's migration. Critic correction (binding): Restate it as: MCP has no recovery path for an abandoned apply (owner-only finish, no TTL), and its plan map grows without bound. A lease TTL tied to the armed timer belongs in the shared design, because the Web has the same retention.
  - `E-06` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) The global migrations mutex is held across router commit and rollback. Evidence: `crates/kcc-mcp/src/migration_tools/finish.rs:66,83-94,103`. Fix: Wrap each `Migration` in `Arc<tokio::sync::Mutex<…>>`. Take the map lock only to clone the entry handle, then lock the entry for the I/O. Critic correction (binding): This is not a guideline violation. It is a contention issue on an uncommon path. A per-entry lock is the fix.
  - `W2-02` (medium; critic adjusted) Wizard session eviction breaks per-run mutual exclusion; any user with `Migrate` can trigger it. Evidence: `wizard/visit.rs:54-59`, `wizard/service.rs:296-301`, `migrate_wizard/launch.rs:52-85`. Fix: Load and check the record's owner before calling `wizards.session(id)`, then take the turn and re-read. Evict only sessions with `Arc::strong_count(session) == 1` and `live == None`. Critic correction (binding): Evidence is at `wizard/service.rs:73-79` and `wizard/resume.rs:114`; the 409 is at `finish.rs:213-215`. The fix stands as proposed.
  - `W5-12` (low; critic confirmed) Up to 64 sessions are shared by all users; eviction drops a session another request is still using. Evidence: `wizard/service.rs:73-79`, `visit.rs:128`. Fix: Check the owner before creating a session, and skip evicting a session whose `turn` is locked (`try_lock`).
  - `W5-06` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) Any Migrate user can create unlimited run directories, and every landing page reads them all. Evidence: `migrate_wizard/landing.rs:174-238`, `presets_page/landing.rs:190-212`, `wizard/store.rs:151-160`. Fix: Cap open runs per owner, prune finished or abandoned runs past a retention window (this also fixes W5-03), and keep an owner index instead of scanning everything.
  Must NOT: change unrelated behaviour, add a crate that is not already in `[workspace.dependencies]` or `Cargo.lock`, weaken a security control, change the audit entry format or hash rule, or touch files owned by another open todo of the same wave beyond what this todo needs; keep routes, MCP tool names/schemas and CLI flags except where a finding above changes them on purpose (name each such change in the commit body).
  Closes: GAP-10

- [ ] 34. Wizard secrets are kept only as long as needed
  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings: sealed sources and typed passwords are deleted when the run finishes or expires (all origins, N16), cleared when source or target changes (W5-04), and passwords are mapped by the user name, not the row index (W5-16). Update docs/PRIVACY.md. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `W5-03` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) Migrations keep the whole sealed source config and the typed passwords forever. Evidence: `migrate_wizard/finish.rs:21-64`, `wizard/finishing.rs:86-87`, `presets_page/finish.rs:69-71`, `migrate_wizard/landing.rs:212-216`. Fix: Override `Finishing::discard` for the migration record (remove `Source` and `Passwords`). Check the age in `PresetRun::preset` for `Origin::Copy`. Add a retention sweep for run directories. Critic correction (binding): Also affected: runs of saved presets (`Origin::Preset`) abandoned before step 8 keep `passwords.sealed` forever, because `sweep` (`saved.rs:135`) expires only `Origin::Copy`. The fix stands: an override of `Finishing::discard` for migrations, plus an age-based sweep of every run's sealed parts.
  - `W5-04` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) Changing source or target clears the password list but keeps `passwords.sealed`; old passwords are reused silently. Evidence: `migrate_wizard/record.rs:266-276`, `target.rs:231-232`, `source.rs:265`, `wizard/visit.rs:184-191`. Fix: `store.discard(id, Sealed::Passwords)` wherever `forget_target_choices` runs (`choose_source` / `choose_target` in both wizards), or derive "stored" from one source only. Critic correction (binding): Limit the claim to the presets wizard (`presets_page/inputs.rs:94` vs `:139-145`). In the migration wizard the only inconsistency is that `record.passwords` is cleared while the sealed map, which the UI shows as stored, survives.
  - `N16` (low; second critic confirmed) Saved-preset runs abandoned before the last step keep `passwords.sealed` forever (`presets_page/saved.rs:129-141` sweeps only `Origin::Copy`). Binding description: `sweep` expires only `Origin::Copy` records, and `discard` runs only on finish. A saved-preset run abandoned midway therefore keeps `passwords.sealed` indefinitely. It is encrypted under the master key, so exposure needs that key, and PRIVACY.md promises cleanup only for copy runs. This is the same class as W5-03 (migrations) but a separate code path.
  - `W5-16` (low; critic confirmed) Migration passwords are mapped by row index; the hidden user-name field is ignored. Evidence: `templates/partials/migrate_inputs.html`, `inputs_submit.rs:147-148`. Fix: Key the password fields by user name, or check `user-N` against `needs.users()[N]`.

- [ ] 35. Poller and background sessions reuse logins and shut down cleanly
  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings: the read-only polling login keeps one session per router across rounds and logs out on shutdown; background operations reuse the parent's key cache and log out when their handle finishes or is dropped (from a spawned task) (A-07); fix the poller idle-exit race. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `SEC2-03` (medium; critic confirmed) A profile with a read-only login opens a new router session (and ndw4 Argon2) on every poll and never logs out. Evidence: `crates/kcc-app/src/registry.rs:646-655`, `poller.rs:358`, `poller.rs:29`, `poller/extended.rs:78`. Fix: Cache the read-only client per id like `client()` does (invalidate on reload/update), and use it in both `poll_client` and `poll_info`.
  - `B-06` (medium; critic confirmed) With a read-only login, the poller opens a new router session every round. Evidence: `crates/kcc-app/src/registry.rs:646-653`, `poller.rs:358`, `poller/extended.rs:78`. Fix: Cache the read-only client per router beside `clients`, and invalidate it on the same profile changes.
  - `A-07` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) Every background operation logs in afresh and never logs out. Evidence: `crates/kcc-rci/src/session.rs:502`. Fix: Reuse the parent's key cache (or `Arc<Cache>`). Call `logout` when the Handle finishes or is dropped (from a spawned task). Use `encode_query(&[("id", &process_id)])`. Add a distinct cancellation error.
  - `B-17` (low; critic confirmed) Poller idle-exit race can hand out a receiver that never gets data. Evidence: `poller.rs:318-323`. Fix: Re-check `receiver_count()` while holding the `channels` lock before removing the entry.

- [ ] 36. Jobs and backups: no leaked lock files, no silent record loss, retention survives bad files
  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings. B-21: skip unreadable backup files during listing and retention (do not move them) and report them separately (a count in the listing and one warning log per file), so retention keeps working. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `B-09` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) Every job leaves two lock files in the state root forever. Evidence: `crates/kcc-app/src/jobs.rs:601`, `jobs/job_ownership.rs:89-91`, `persist.rs:289-291`, `jobs.rs:648-655`. Fix: Drop the per-record lock (ownership already serializes writers) and remove the `job-owner-*.lock` file when pruning the finished record.
  - `B-10` (low; critic confirmed) A failed final write of a job record is dropped silently, and the next start reports a false interruption. Evidence: `crates/kcc-app/src/jobs.rs:476`. Fix: Log the error with `job_error_kind` at minimum.

  - `B-21` (low; critic confirmed) One malformed backup file turns off retention for that router permanently. Evidence: `backups/store.rs:221`, `tests/backups.rs:201-233`. Fix: Skip or quarantine unreadable entries during listing and retention, and report them separately.

- [ ] 37. No blocking file, lock or crypto work on async workers
  What to do / Must NOT do: Move blocking file I/O, fsync, `flock` waits and preset crypto to `spawn_blocking` (or tokio fs) in kcc-app, the wizard store and the preset store; B-07's 'release `clients` before connecting' part is already done by todo 20's per-id OnceCell (SEC2-09) - do not implement it twice; G-22 also removes the duplicated PresetStore method. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `B-07` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) Blocking file I/O and blocking `flock` waits run directly on Tokio workers. Evidence: `crates/kcc-app/src/workflow.rs:1370-1374`, `backups/store.rs:85,101`, `registry.rs:359,525,571,606`, `jobs.rs:427,476,524`. Fix: Wrap these synchronous store calls in `spawn_blocking`, as `kcc-web` already does for accounts. Release `clients` before connecting, then insert with a re-check. Critic correction (binding): This is a guideline violation with small, bounded stalls. Keep the fix (`spawn_blocking` for the store calls, and connect outside the `clients` lock).
  - `W2-08` (low; critic confirmed) The wizard store does blocking file I/O, including fsync, in async handlers. Evidence: `wizard/store.rs:648-652`, `visit.rs:59`, `visit.rs:105`, `finish.rs:275`. Fix: Wrap the `Store` calls in `spawn_blocking`, the way `config_page::store::blocking` does.
  - `W5-05` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) Wizard file I/O, including fsync, runs on the async workers. Evidence: `wizard/store.rs:324-328`, `migrate_wizard/inputs_submit.rs:85-96`, `store.rs:141-169`, `landing.rs:136`. Fix: Make the `Store` methods async over `spawn_blocking`, or wrap them in a `spawn_blocking` helper. Critic correction (binding): none to the facts; the severity is lowered because the cost per request is bounded and small.
  - `G-22` (low; critic confirmed) `PresetStore` duplicates a method and does blocking file and crypto I/O on async handlers. Evidence: `presets/store.rs:194-199`, `kcc-mcp/src/preset_tools/capture.rs:190-193`, `kcc-web/src/presets_page/compose.rs:148`. Fix: drop one of the two methods, call the store through `spawn_blocking` (or `tokio::fs`), and skip and log bad records in `list`.

- [ ] 38. Web resource bounds: diagnostics polling, log streams, global caps, read-only views, review submit
  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `C-19` (low; critic confirmed) Diagnostics poll the router every 10 ms. Evidence: `crates/kcc-domain/src/diagnostics.rs:212`, `kcc-rci/src/engine/background.rs:100-106,129`. Fix: Keep the default interval in production and shorten it only in tests (or use a paused clock).
  - `W4-13` (low; critic confirmed) Each log-page stream polls the router itself. Evidence: `log_source.rs:25-26`, `log_source.rs:67-71`, `logs.rs:163-168`. Fix: Share one poller per router (a `watch`/broadcast like `JobFeeds`), or document that the limit is per viewer.
  - `W4-07` (low; critic confirmed) Global caps evict other sessions' downloads and running jobs' answers. Evidence: `advanced.rs:51-55`, `advanced.rs:129-134`, `job_stream.rs:144-150`. Fix: Bound per session or user, not globally. Evict only finished jobs' slots, or keep the slot inside the job closure until a listener reads it.
  - `W2-07` (low; critic adjusted) Read-only page views do far more work than needed. Evidence: `config_page/store.rs:233-241`, `backups.rs:110-114`, `table/rows.rs:115-116`, `config_page/compare.rs:185-187`. Fix: Take the size from file metadata (or record it in `BackupInfo`). Use `sort_by_cached_key`. Run the diff in `spawn_blocking`. Critic correction (binding): The decryption is off the async runtime (`spawn_blocking`). The real costs are needless decryption per page view, and a CPU-bound diff on the async worker (§5 "use `yield_now`/`spawn_blocking`").
  - `W5-11` (low; critic confirmed) A user-controlled form size makes review submit quadratic on the async worker. Evidence: `migrate_wizard/review_submit.rs:138-144`, `page_kit/request.rs:69-75`, `plan.rs:49-59`. Fix: Collect the form once into a `BTreeMap<&str, &str>` (last value wins) before the loop.

- [ ] 39. RCI session and authentication robustness
  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings: cookie refresh stores only the session cookie from successful responses to requests sent with the current cookie (A-02); an unrelated non-ASCII `Set-Cookie` is ignored, not fatal (N5); digest-proxy state is safe under concurrency; router-supplied Argon2 iterations are capped locally (A-14: add `Options::max_argon2_iterations`, which like `max_argon2_memory_kib` may only lower the protocol maximum of 64, refused with `AuthError::CostTooHigh`; it comes from a new `kcc.toml` setting `[outbound] argon2_max_iterations` (default 16, accepted range 1..=64, passed through the registry into the RCI client options) so an operator whose router needs more can raise it without a rebuild; `OutboundConfig` derives `Default` under `#[serde(default)]` (`crates/kcc-app/src/settings.rs:209-219`), so remove `Default` from its derive list and give the field its default of 16 through a manual `Default` impl for `OutboundConfig` (a derived `u32` default of 0 would be out of range; any serde field default function must return the same 16, because a config without `[outbound]` and the test call sites use `OutboundConfig::default()`); the only struct literal (`crates/kcc-app/src/registry/transition_tests.rs:41-44`) already ends with `..OutboundConfig::default()` and needs no change; document it in docs/CONFIG.md and in `kcc.example.toml` as `argon2_max_iterations = 16`, the default (the existing test `crates/kcc-app/tests/example_config.rs:48` asserts that the example's `[outbound]` equals `Config::default().outbound`), add a CHANGELOG line; tests: iter 16 accepted and 17 refused at the default, 17 accepted when the setting is 17, a setting outside 1..=64 refused at config load; note in docs/BACKLOG.md that the default is unverified on real 5.2 hardware); timeouts map to one error on every auth path. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `A-02` (medium; critic confirmed) Cookie refresh writes any `Set-Cookie` into the current session, including on 401 and from requests sent with an older cookie. Evidence: see the finding text. Fix: Refresh only on non-401 responses, and only while the current cookie `same_cookie(&snapshot)`. Never turn a refresh-parse failure into an error on a response that was already applied.
  - `N5` (low; second critic confirmed) `parse_set_cookie` (`crates/kcc-rci/src/session.rs:771-777`) fails the whole response on any unrelated non-ASCII `Set-Cookie`. Binding description: `parse_set_cookie` runs `raw.to_str()?` (`:772`) on every `Set-Cookie` before the name filter (`:777`). One non-visible-ASCII cookie therefore makes `refresh_session` fail after `send_with_session` has already returned. A POST the router applied is then reported as `RciError::Transport`, and the front ends audit it as a failure. The trigger needs the router, or a proxy in front of it, to send such a cookie. Nothing in the repo shows Keenetic doing this, so the path is reachable but unlikely.
  - `A-09` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) Digest proxy state is not safe under concurrent requests. Evidence: `crates/kcc-rci/src/auth_digest.rs:79-104`. Fix: Under the lock, reuse the stored challenge when the nonce is the same; otherwise replace it. Do not reset the count for an identical nonce.
  - `A-14` (low; critic confirmed) Router-controlled Argon2 iterations are not capped locally. Evidence: `crates/kcc-rci/src/ndw4_reply.rs:110-120`, `crates/kcc-rci/src/ndw4_keys.rs:18`. Fix: Add a local `max_argon2_iterations` (or a combined memory×iterations budget) to `Options` and refuse with `CostTooHigh`.
  - `A-15` (low; critic adjusted) Timeout errors are mapped differently on each auth path. Evidence: `crates/kcc-rci/src/auth_token.rs:24`, `auth_digest.rs:87,105`, `auth_ndw4.rs:166,189`, `session.rs:801-807`. Fix: Use `map_reqwest_error` everywhere (make it `pub(crate)`). Critic correction (binding): Drop the `RequestOutcome` mislabelling claim.
  Must NOT: change unrelated behaviour, add a crate that is not already in `[workspace.dependencies]` or `Cargo.lock`, weaken a security control, change the audit entry format or hash rule, or touch files owned by another open todo of the same wave beyond what this todo needs; keep routes, MCP tool names/schemas and CLI flags except where a finding above changes them on purpose (name each such change in the commit body).
  Closes: GAP-3
  Parallelization: Wave W5 | Blocked by: 38 | Blocks: 40 | Can parallelize with: 45
  References (executor has NO interview context): `.omo/reviews/kcc-full-review-2026-11-15/VALIDATED.md`, `REPORT.md` (same directory); finding texts: `.omo/reviews/kcc-full-review-2026-11-15/findings/A.md`; critic verdicts: `critics/A.md`, `critics/SECOND-H1-H4.md`, `critics/SECOND-H5-H7.md`, `critics/SECOND-NEW.md`; every `path:line` in the finding lines above (line numbers are from HEAD `349b7fb`; trust the quoted code over the number).
  Acceptance criteria (agent-executable): for EVERY finding above that describes wrong behaviour, a regression test that names the finding id in its doc comment fails before the fix and passes after (evidence shows the red run, then the green run); for structural, dead-code, doc or comment findings, the evidence shows the corrected code or a grep proving the old pattern is gone; `cargo test -p kcc-rci -p kcc-app -p kcc --all-features` exits 0; G0 green (plus `cargo xtask i18n-check` when strings change, `cargo xtask cancel-safety` when async fns change, `cargo xtask assets-check` when templates or assets change).

- [ ] 40. RCI engine correctness and hygiene
  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings: `show` keeps the query on the POST fallback and caches per path+method correctly; `batch` checks the reply length and returns completed chunks with the error; status items that do not deserialize are reported as errors; `connect_bootstrap_with` keeps the observer; one place for the magic numbers; delete dead API (A-18's contradictory cancel-safety comments are fixed in todo 66); `FirmwareVersion` derives `PartialOrd, Ord` (behaviour identical; the test proves the ordering of every field, including the `Stage` variant order, `crates/kcc-rci/src/version.rs:53-75`). Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `A-03` (medium; critic confirmed) `RciClient::show` drops the query on the POST fallback and caches the path as POST-only. Evidence: `crates/kcc-rci/src/engine.rs:399-412`. Fix: POST the serialized query as the body: build the POST body from `query` (`serde_json::to_value(query)`) instead of `{}`, or refuse the fallback when the query is not empty.
  - `A-04` (medium; critic adjusted) `batch` never checks the reply length and drops completed chunks on a later error. Evidence: `crates/kcc-rci/src/engine.rs:429-449`, `crates/kcc-app/src/workflow_batch.rs:52-70`. Fix: Return `Decode` (or a typed partial error that carries the results so far) when `values.len() != chunk.len()`. On a mid-batch failure, return the completed results together with the error. Critic correction (binding): Drop the `stop_on_error` impact. The concrete consequence is an applied but unaudited multi-chunk batch on a later-chunk failure, plus silent miscounting if the reply is short.
  - `A-05` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) Status items that do not fully deserialize are dropped, so error-level replies count as success. Evidence: see the finding text. Fix: Decide the level from the `status` member alone. Make `code`, `ident` and `message` lenient (string or number, optional). Treat any unrecognized level as an error. Critic correction (binding): This is defense-in-depth. Decide the level from `status` alone and treat unknown levels as errors. The claim that rejected writes are reported as applied is inferred only.
  - `A-12` (low; critic adjusted) `connect_bootstrap_with` loses the observer, and request accounting differs per method. Evidence: `crates/kcc-rci/src/engine.rs:189-194`. Fix: Chain `.with_observer(client.options.observer.clone())` in bootstrap and background sessions. Route all request paths through one helper that takes the permit and reports. Critic correction (binding): Missing login metrics are limited to bootstrap and background sessions.
  - `A-16` (low; critic confirmed) Duplicated magic numbers and helpers. Evidence: `auth_options.rs:47`, `session.rs:315`, `ndw4_reply.rs:117`, `engine.rs:31`. Fix: One shared constant or helper per item, exported from the owning crate.
  - `A-17` (low; critic adjusted) Dead or speculative API shipped in production. Evidence: `engine.rs:72-73`, `session.rs:194-223`, `kcc-catalog/src/validate.rs:160-169`, `config_text.rs:384-386`. Fix: Delete them, or move them to `#[cfg(test)]` or testkit. Critic correction (binding): Losing the TLS pin means a refused connection, not a weakened one.
  - `A-19` (info; critic adjusted, low->info) `FirmwareVersion` mixes derived equality with hand-written ordering. Evidence: `crates/kcc-rci/src/version.rs:57`. Fix: `#[derive(PartialOrd, Ord)]` and delete the manual impls.
  Must NOT: change unrelated behaviour, add a crate that is not already in `[workspace.dependencies]` or `Cargo.lock`, weaken a security control, change the audit entry format or hash rule, or touch files owned by another open todo of the same wave beyond what this todo needs; keep routes, MCP tool names/schemas and CLI flags except where a finding above changes them on purpose (name each such change in the commit body).
  Closes: GAP-3, GAP-11
  Parallelization: Wave W5 | Blocked by: 39 | Blocks: 41, 42 | Can parallelize with: 45
  References (executor has NO interview context): `.omo/reviews/kcc-full-review-2026-11-15/VALIDATED.md`, `REPORT.md` (same directory); finding texts: `.omo/reviews/kcc-full-review-2026-11-15/findings/A.md`; critic verdicts: `critics/A.md`; every `path:line` in the finding lines above (line numbers are from HEAD `349b7fb`; trust the quoted code over the number).

- [ ] 41. Catalog: DELETE risk, bounded suggestions, typed arguments, firewall reorder
  What to do / Must NOT do: DELETE risk is enforced in the catalog itself and the three front-end patches are removed (A-08); bound the edit-distance work (A-11); generated argument types get constraints instead of a bare `String` where the manual defines them (A-20); N9/D-10: the generator already sees `new-index` but reports "argument new-index has no recognized type" (`catalog/5.1/access-list.json:1315`); make the smallest generator change (Metis P0.5): delete the one entry `{"path":"access-list rule","name":"new-index"}` from `catalog/untyped-arguments.json` and map the exact argument name `new-index` to Integer in `inferred_value_type` (`xtask/src/catalog_build.rs:929-951`). `catalog/.cache/` (800 MB) is git-ignored, so run this todo in the main checkout or symlink `catalog/.cache` into the worktree. First prove the build is reproducible: at HEAD run `cargo xtask catalog build --version 5.1` (and 5.0, 4.3, one version per run) and require a zero `git diff`; then apply the change, regenerate the three versions, and commit the regenerated catalog and `crates/kcc-catalog/src/generated.rs` together. Also run `cargo test --manifest-path xtask/Cargo.toml --all-features` and `cargo clippy --manifest-path xtask/Cargo.toml --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`, so firewall reorder can be catalog-validated (used by todo 49); E-22: classify unvalidated raw CLI lines as Destructive (they then need `[mcp] allow_destructive = true` and `confirm: true`), regenerate `docs/MCP_TOOLS.md` with `cargo xtask gen-tool-docs`, and add a CHANGELOG line. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `A-08` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) DELETE risk is not enforced by the catalog; three frontends patch it themselves. Evidence: `crates/kcc-catalog/src/request.rs:48-59`, `crates/kcc-catalog/src/runtime.rs:457-468`, `crates/kcc-catalog/src/request.rs:189-193`, `kcc-mcp/src/generic/rci.rs:270-274`. Fix: In `ValidatedRequest::capture`, raise the risk to `Destructive` when `method == Delete`, then drop the three frontend patches.
  - `A-11` (low; critic confirmed) Unbounded edit-distance work on attacker-controlled field names. Evidence: see the finding text. Fix: Skip the suggestion when `field.len()` exceeds the longest sibling plus 2, which already guarantees a distance above 2.
  - `A-20` (low; critic adjusted) Generated argument types fall back to an unconstrained `String`. Evidence: `crates/kcc-catalog/src/runtime/data.rs:267-277`. Fix: Map `"String" | "Filename"` explicitly and return `InvalidGeneratedCatalog` for unknown types. Critic correction (binding): The lines are `data.rs:198-207`. Return an error for unknown strings instead of citing the enum rule.
  - `N9` (info; second critic adjusted) The catalog has no type for `access-list rule ... order <new-index>` ("argument new-index has no recognized type"). Binding description: This is already tracked through the untyped-argument allowlist. The only cleanup is to remove `RuleProperty::Order` or type `new-index`.
  - `E-22` (info; critic confirmed) The unvalidated CLI escape is not recorded as a deviation from project rule (b). Evidence: `crates/kcc-mcp/src/generic/rci.rs:433-439`, `kcc-catalog/src/request.rs:49-50`. Fix: List it as an open deviation in the project overlay, or classify unvalidated lines as Destructive.

- [ ] 43. MCP results: truncation keeps codes, `fields` projection works, cursors are meaningful
  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings: truncation keeps `error`, codes and fail-safe messages and returns a usable cursor; apply the `fields` projection from the input; `limit` is clamped to `1..=100` as the migration tools do (`finish.rs:112`, E-20); input schemas describe `confirm` correctly. Regenerate `docs/MCP_TOOLS.md` with `cargo xtask gen-tool-docs` (staleness test `crates/kcc-mcp/tests/domain_tools.rs:174-181`). Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `E-04` (medium; critic confirmed) Result truncation replaces the whole payload, including error codes and fail-safe messages, and returns a meaningless cursor. Evidence: `crates/kcc-mcp/src/support/shape.rs:191-203`, `kcc-app/src/settings.rs:202`. Fix: Always keep `error`, `message`, `field`, `unsaved`, `persists_immediately` and `audit_pending`, and truncate only the bulky array. Emit a real next offset only for tools that take `cursor`.
  - `E-12` (low; critic confirmed) The `fields` projection argument is accepted and validated but never applied. Evidence: `crates/kcc-mcp/src/support/shape.rs:174-183`, `generic/fleet.rs:39-41,58-60`, `generic/rci.rs:57-59`. Fix: Remove `fields` from the schemas and from `shape`, or pass the parsed input into `shape` and project the result's item arrays.
  - `E-20` (low; critic confirmed) `catalog_search` with `limit: 0` returns a `next_cursor` equal to the current cursor. Evidence: `crates/kcc-mcp/src/generic/fleet.rs:144,155`. Fix: Clamp the limit to `1..=100`, as the migration tools already do (`finish.rs:112`).
  - `E-18` (low; critic confirmed) Input schemas call `confirm` "unused", but it is required for some writes. Evidence: `crates/kcc-mcp/src/domains/write/filtering.rs:96-98`, `common.rs:117`, `tests/support/runtime_bracket.rs:167-172`, `filtering.rs:119-124`. Fix: Document `confirm` as "required when the change touches management", and drop `index`/`description`.

- [ ] 44. Client pseudonymization covers names, free text and notifications, bounded per router
  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings: redact_clients covers client names, free text and progress notifications; the pseudonymizer matches IP fields exactly (not the substring "ip"); both pseudonymizers (MCP E-13 and metrics G-17) use a keyed hash with a per-process random key instead of a growing map - use std's `std::hash::RandomState` (SipHash with a random key), no new crate. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `E-05` (medium; critic confirmed) `redact_clients_in_mcp` misses client names, free text and progress notifications. Evidence: `crates/kcc-mcp/src/support/shape.rs:271-278`, `kcc-domain/src/clients.rs:38`, `domains/write/jobs.rs:70-74`, `kcc-domain/src/diagnostics.rs:94-98`. Fix: Add `name` under client objects. Scrub MAC and IP patterns inside strings. Pass progress messages through the same pseudonymizer.
  - `E-13` (low; critic confirmed) The pseudonymizer over-matches on the substring "ip", and its map grows without bound. Evidence: `crates/kcc-mcp/src/support/shape.rs:258-264,274-276`, `lib.rs:244`, `generic/router.rs:85`. Fix: Match whole key tokens (`key == "ip"` or split on `-`/`_`), and bound or session-scope the map.
  - `G-17` (low; critic adjusted) The per-client pseudonym map grows without bound and is shared across routers. Evidence: `metrics/clients.rs:180-185`, `collector.rs:44-45`. Fix: keep pseudonyms only for MACs present in the current snapshot (rebuild each scrape), or hash the MAC with a per-process key. Critic correction (binding): This is an unbounded-memory and identifier-retention issue, not a rule (i) violation. Prefer a keyed hash (HMAC with a per-process key).

- [ ] 45. Log forwarding is reliable and bounded as documented (M47-M50)
  What to do / Must NOT do: Retry transport errors with capped exponential backoff; per-request timeouts on both sinks; bounded Elasticsearch reply body; make the code match docs/MONITORING.md "Data loss" (at-least-once while kcc runs: transport errors are retried like 429/5xx with the same 60-second delay cap; a 10-second flush at exit; records left after it and queue overflow are counted in `kcc_log_records_dropped_total`), and add malformed router records (G-10) to that section; cancellable sleeps so shutdown does not wait router by router; batch selection without re-encoding per record and without holding the std Mutex during encoding; one malformed record is counted and skipped, not blocking; router-local timestamps are labelled as naive local time (no extra router command) instead of being stamped UTC; the Loki `level` label is mapped to a fixed set. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `G-08` (medium; critic confirmed) Log-sink transport errors are not retried; the batch is dropped as "permanent". Evidence: `crates/kcc-telemetry/src/logs/sink.rs:290-305`, `pipeline_delivery.rs:216-219`, `elastic_sink.rs:188`. Fix: treat `Transport` (and `BulkError::Delivery(Transport)`) as retriable.
  - `G-09` (medium; critic confirmed) No HTTP timeout and unbounded retries: a stuck sink stalls the worker, and shutdown aborts records without counting them. Evidence: `sink.rs:76`, `elastic_sink.rs:76`, `pipeline_delivery.rs:199-209`, `pipeline.rs:91-101`. Fix: set request and connect timeouts on both clients. Select `worker.cancellation.cancelled()` against `clock.wait` and `send`. On abort, count the pending and in-flight records as `dropped{reason="shutdown"}`.
  - `SEC2-14` (low; critic confirmed) Log-sink clients have no timeouts, and the Elasticsearch reply body is unbounded. Evidence: `crates/kcc-telemetry/src/logs/elastic_sink.rs:76`, `sink.rs:76`, `loki_reply.rs:10`. Fix: Set `connect_timeout` and `timeout` on both builders, and read the Bulk reply through a capped reader like `loki_reply::read_body`.
  - `G-12` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) Log-collector shutdown waits out uncancellable sleeps, one router after another. Evidence: `service.rs:201`, `runtime.rs:114-116`. Fix: wrap each sleep in `select!` with `cancellation.cancelled()`, and cancel all collectors before awaiting them (`join_all`). Critic correction (binding): Treat as the root cause of BACKLOG "Known gaps" `server.rs:203-206`, plus the per-router multiplication.
  - `G-13` (medium; critic confirmed) Batch selection re-encodes the whole batch for every record while holding the queue's std `Mutex`. Evidence: `pipeline_delivery.rs:157-162`. Fix: add each record's encoded size incrementally (plus a fixed envelope), and pop the batch under the lock but measure it outside.
  - `G-10` (medium; critic confirmed) One malformed router log record silently blocks all forwarding for that router. Evidence: `crates/kcc-telemetry/src/logs/service.rs:285-287`, `parser.rs:42-48`. Fix: parse per record, skip and count the bad ones (`log_records_dropped("collector","parse")`), and keep the rest.
  - `G-11` (medium; critic confirmed) Router-local log timestamps are stamped as UTC. Evidence: `service.rs:280-284`, `parser.rs:130`, `types.rs:13`, `tests/logs.rs:60`. Fix: obtain the router's UTC offset (for example from `show clock date`) per collector and pass it to `parse_records`. Otherwise label the field "naive local time" and do not claim UTC.
  - `G-24` (info; critic adjusted, low->info) The Loki `level` stream label comes straight from untrusted router input. Evidence: `logs/parser.rs:73-76`, `format.rs:62`. Fix: map `level` to a fixed set (error, warning, info, debug, other).
  Must NOT: change unrelated behaviour, add a crate that is not already in `[workspace.dependencies]` or `Cargo.lock`, weaken a security control, change the audit entry format or hash rule, or touch files owned by another open todo of the same wave beyond what this todo needs; keep routes, MCP tool names/schemas and CLI flags except where a finding above changes them on purpose (name each such change in the commit body).

- [ ] 48. DHCP pools: parsing, DNS servers, defaults and overwrite protection (M35)
  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings: `parse_pools` stops at the next top-level block; pools refuse more than one DNS server with a field error (the catalog types only `address1`; the generator's mis-parse of `address2` goes to docs/BACKLOG.md) and clear DNS with `no ip dhcp pool <name> dns-server`; the CLI does not default the gateway to the first leased address and does not wipe options on replace; a 'new' pool cannot overwrite an existing one; read-back runs before the audit entry says success (C-23). Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `C-08` (medium; critic confirmed) `parse_pools` attaches indented lines of later blocks to the last DHCP pool. Evidence: `crates/kcc-domain/src/dhcp/model.rs:175-187`. Fix: Clear the current pool on any non-indented line that is not an `ip dhcp pool` header.
  - `C-09` (medium; critic adjusted) `plan_pool` silently drops every DNS server after the first. Evidence: `crates/kcc-domain/src/dhcp.rs:107-112`, `dhcp/model.rs:140-143`, `kcc-web/src/clients_pages/dhcp_forms.rs:140`. Fix: Refuse more than the catalog supports (`address1` only), or render all of them. Critic correction (binding): The reachable path is `kcc dhcp pool … --dns A --dns B`, not the Web UI.
  - `W3-04` (medium; critic confirmed) DHCP pool editor keeps only the first DNS server, cannot clear DNS, and lets a "new" pool overwrite an existing one. Evidence: `clients_pages/dhcp_forms.rs:86`, `kcc-domain/src/dhcp.rs:107-112`, `dhcp_forms.rs:74-75`. Fix: Carry every DNS server through the form (or two fields). Emit `no … dns-server` when the field is empty. In `save_pool`, refuse a name that is already taken when `existing` is absent, using `submit_reading` to check against `dhcp::read_pools`.
  - `F-11` (medium; critic adjusted) `dhcp pool` defaults the default router to the first leased address and wipes options on replace. Evidence: `crates/kcc/src/cli/dom_lan.rs:106-110`, `args_dom_lan.rs:46`, `kcc-web/src/clients_pages/dhcp_forms.rs:136-144`. Fix: Make `--gateway` required (as the Web does), or default it to the pool interface's own address. Refuse to replace an existing pool without the options the user omitted. Critic correction (binding): Drop "clears the DNS servers". The harmful default is the gateway, and the replace resets only lease and state.
  - `C-23` (low; critic adjusted) Client and DHCP read-back runs after the audit entry says "success". Evidence: `clients.rs:226-227`, `dhcp.rs:170-193`, `workflow.rs:1151-1153`. Fix: Pass these checks as `VerificationSpec`s into `apply_cli`.

- [ ] 49. Firewall planner and reader correctness
  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings: FQDN group description works; `plan_rule` refuses ports unless the protocol is TCP or UDP, and the test matrix asserts the refusal; one unparsable rule is reported, not fatal to every write (and never after the write); D-07: document the read-back limit for an already-existing target in the rustdoc and docs/BACKLOG.md (the critic's narrowed scope); reorder goes through `RuleProperty::Order` and the catalog argument from todo 41, and the Web stops building its own line (W3-08). Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `D-03` (medium; critic confirmed) Setting an FQDN group description is always refused. Evidence: `crates/kcc-domain/src/dns/change.rs:172-175`, `kcc-catalog/src/validate.rs:225-230`. Fix: Validate `{"description"}` only and carry `name` outside the validated value, as `FqdnInclude` does. Add a test.
  - `D-05` (medium; critic confirmed) Ports are rendered for ICMP/ESP/GRE/IPIP/IP firewall rules, and the test matrix accepts it. Evidence: `firewall/change.rs:193-202`, `crates/kcc-mcp/src/domains/write/filtering.rs:214,227`, `tests/firewall_render.rs:25,117-121`. Fix: Refuse ports unless the protocol is TCP or UDP, either in `plan_rule` or by making `Rule` carry ports only for TCP/UDP. Drop those cells from the matrix, or assert the refusal.
  - `D-06` (medium; critic confirmed) One unparsable rule makes every CLI/MCP firewall write fail, after the write. Evidence: `crates/kcc-domain/src/firewall/read.rs:48-51`, `read.rs:219`, `change.rs:181-189`. Fix: Parse only the targeted list, and keep unknown forms as "unstructured" entries instead of failing, as `rule_words::StoredRule` does.
  - `D-07` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) Read-back cannot detect a no-op when the target already exists. Evidence: `firewall/change/verify.rs:98-100,129-133`, `vpn_clients/import.rs:172-176`, `vpn_clients/secrets.rs:154-158`. Fix: Snapshot before applying. For secrets, compare the opaque `ns3` payload before and after; it must change, and needs no decoding. For rules, compare the count before and after. Critic correction (binding): This is a weakness in verification strength on re-set or duplicate paths, not a broken flow. For secrets, document it as a known limit, or compare payloads only when the old and new secret are known to differ.
  - `D-10` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) `RuleProperty::Order` can never be planned, and the Web builds its own order line. Evidence: `firewall/change.rs:107`, `crates/kcc-web/src/firewall_page/lines.rs:80-82`. Fix: Map `Order` to the catalog's `new-index` argument and route the Web through it, or delete the variant. Critic correction (binding): The cost is a dead public variant plus a hand-built Web line, with no unsafe value. Mapping to `new-index` first needs the catalog generator to emit that argument. Otherwise, delete the variant.
  - `W3-08` (low; critic confirmed) Firewall rule reorder builds its CLI line in the web layer, with a validated leaf that doesn't match the line. Evidence: `firewall_page/lines.rs:80-83`, `lines.rs:65`. Fix: Add `plan_order_rule` to the firewall domain next to `plan_rule_property`, call it from here, and drop `_words`.
  Must NOT: change unrelated behaviour, add a crate that is not already in `[workspace.dependencies]` or `Cargo.lock`, weaken a security control, change the audit entry format or hash rule, or touch files owned by another open todo of the same wave beyond what this todo needs; keep routes, MCP tool names/schemas and CLI flags except where a finding above changes them on purpose (name each such change in the commit body).

- [ ] 50. DNS, policy, route and segment planner fixes
  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings: clearing a policy description works; route removal is verified by the specific route, and the wipe recovery restores every removed route; segment create rolls back the failing step; a stray `kcc-segment:` description no longer breaks discovery. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `D-04` (medium; critic confirmed) Clearing a connection-policy description always fails. Evidence: `crates/kcc-domain/src/policies/change/plan.rs:36`, `crates/kcc-domain/src/policies/change/render.rs:46-49`. Fix: When `no` is set, render `no ip policy N description` without an argument.
  - `D-08` (medium; critic confirmed) Route removal is checked by routing-table row count, and the wipe recovery is lossy. Evidence: `crates/kcc-domain/src/routing/change.rs:83-104`, `change/ipv6.rs:95-118`. Fix: Verify absence of the exact command in `show rc`. Take the restore set from the running config, not the table, and restore every route before returning the first error.
  - `D-09` (medium; critic confirmed) Segment create does not roll back the step that failed. Evidence: `crates/kcc-domain/src/segments.rs:206-224`, `segments/step.rs:255-265`. Fix: When the step's own request was sent, include that step in the rollback (run its delete line).
  - `D-20` (low; critic confirmed) One stray `kcc-segment:` description breaks segment discovery. Evidence: `crates/kcc-domain/src/segments/discovery.rs:194-211`. Fix: Skip and log a bad record instead of failing the entire read.

- [ ] 51. VPN client and server fixes; no key text in errors
  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings: VPN-client delete only removes VPN interfaces and keeps the catalog's Destructive risk; the crypto-map virtual IP pointer is fixed; WireGuard parse errors never carry key material in Display or Debug (D-16 binding correction, N8: `UnknownField`, `Malformed` and `Duplicate` store only the line number, never text from the line; test: a line `<44-character base64 key>=` and a malformed key line produce errors whose Display and Debug contain neither the key nor the line text); ASC extra values are rejected or ignored as documented. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):
  - `D-11` (medium; critic confirmed) VPN-client Delete removes any interface and drops the catalog's Destructive risk. Evidence: `vpn_clients/render.rs:21-25`, `vpn_clients/change.rs:96-101`, `interfaces/management.rs:92-120`, `kcc-catalog/src/runtime.rs:466-467`. Fix: Read the interface first and refuse names whose `type` is not a VPN client type. Keep `max(catalog risk, classified risk)`.
  - `D-12` (medium; critic confirmed) VPN server status reads the crypto-map virtual IP at the wrong pointer. Evidence: `crates/kcc-domain/src/vpn_servers.rs:208,222-225`, `vpn_servers/verification.rs:37-41`. Fix: Request `crypto map {}` and collect `virtual-ip` per map name.
  - `D-16` (medium; critic adjusted) WireGuard parse error can keep a raw key line in a `Debug`-derived error. Evidence: `crates/kcc-domain/src/vpn_clients/wireguard/parser.rs:68-74`, `wireguard.rs:102`. Fix: Store only the line number for `Malformed`. Critic correction (binding): Store only the line number in `UnknownField`, `Malformed` and `Duplicate` (anything that can hold raw line text), or never derive `field` from unparsed text. This is a project rule (d) violation in Display error text.
  - `N8` (low; second critic confirmed) WireGuard parser errors (`vpn_clients/wireguard/parser.rs:173-176,194-197`) carry key text in `UnknownField.field`/`Malformed`, printed by Display. Binding description: The field name is the text before the first `=`. A bare base64 key line (`<key>=`) therefore becomes `UnknownField { field: <43 key chars> }`. `Malformed` similarly carries the whole line, and Display prints both. The CLI prints Display via `Failure::text`. The Web drops `field` (`vpn_conf.rs` uses only `line`), and MCP offers no import. The secret goes only to the importing operator's own stderr, who already holds the file, though terminal scrollback and CI logs are the cost.
  - `D-17` (medium; critic confirmed) WireGuard ASC: the doc says extra values are ignored, but the line keeps them. Evidence: `vpn_clients.rs:227-228`, `vpn_clients/render.rs:62-66`. Fix: Refuse more than 16 values, and quote each value.
  Must NOT: change unrelated behaviour, add a crate that is not already in `[workspace.dependencies]` or `Cargo.lock`, weaken a security control, change the audit entry format or hash rule, or touch files owned by another open todo of the same wave beyond what this todo needs; keep routes, MCP tool names/schemas and CLI flags except where a finding above changes them on purpose (name each such change in the commit body).

  What to do / Must NOT do: Per the findings: one shared set of planner helpers and one config-text walker; typed input instead of sentinels/string dispatch; delete dead branches and phantom inventory entries; remove test-only production code; replace module/crate-wide `#[expect]` with item-level ones and fix what they hid; C-10: remove partition check and format from the domain, MCP and CLI (the Web already hides them), regenerate `docs/MCP_TOOLS.md` (`cargo xtask gen-tool-docs`) and add a CHANGELOG "Removed" line. Findings closed by this todo (fix each one; where a critic correction is given it overrides the reviewer's text):

Evidence: `firewall/change.rs:267-272`, `policies/change/plan.rs:117-122`, `routing/change.rs:184-196`, `nat/change.rs:337-349`. Fix: One crate-internal `plan_one(router, path, body, risk)` and one typed word renderer. Critic correction (binding): The NAT copies are at `nat/change.rs:121-133` (`plan`) and `156-163` (`push`).
  - `D-13` (low; critic adjusted, medium->low) Hand-written config-text walkers, duplicated and already drifted. Fix: Use `kcc_config::config_text::parse`/`flatten` with one shared "find context and child" helper. Critic correction (binding): This is a maintenance duplication only. The claim that one config could verify as present in one domain and absent in another is not demonstrated.
